  reports_to_position_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the supervisory position to which this role reports.']
  unit_id varchar [note: 'add column department_id (BIGINT) with FK to workforce.department.department_id - positions must belong to a department for org structure']
  classification varchar [note: 'Primary classification indicating whether the role is Back‑of‑House (BOH), Front‑of‑House (FOH), or Support.']
  code varchar [note: 'Business identifier code used to reference the position in HR and scheduling systems.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the position record was first created.']
  description varchar [note: 'Detailed description of duties, responsibilities, and scope of the position.']
  effective_end_date date [note: 'Date when the position is retired or no longer available (nullable).']
  effective_start_date date [note: 'Date when the position becomes active for hiring.']
  flsa_exempt boolean [note: 'True if the position is exempt from Fair Labor Standards Act overtime rules.']
  fte_equivalency decimal(5,2) [note: 'Full‑time equivalent factor used for labor forecasting (e.g., 1.0 = full‑time, 0.5 = half‑time).']
  hourly_rate decimal(10,2) [note: 'Base hourly compensation for hourly‑paid positions.']
  is_manager boolean [note: 'True if the position includes managerial responsibilities.']
  labor_percentage_target decimal(5,2) [note: 'Target labor cost as a percentage of sales for this position.']
  level varchar [note: 'Career level of the position within the organization hierarchy.']
  minimum_age varchar [note: 'Minimum legal age required to be eligible for the position.']
  overtime_eligible boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the position is eligible for overtime pay.']
  pay_band varchar [note: 'Compensation band used for salary or hourly rate determination.']
  position_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle status of the position.']
  required_certifications varchar [note: 'Comma‑separated list of mandatory certifications (e.g., ServSafe, Food Handler).']
  required_experience_years varchar [note: 'Minimum years of relevant experience required for the position.']
  salary_max decimal(10,2) [note: 'Upper bound of the annual salary range for salaried positions.']
  salary_min decimal(10,2) [note: 'Lower bound of the annual salary range for salaried positions.']
  shift_type varchar [note: 'Typical shift pattern associated with the position.']
  title varchar [note: 'Human‑readable name of the job position (e.g., Crew Member, Shift Lead).']
  union_eligible boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the position is eligible for union representation.']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent update to the position record.']

  Note: 'Defines authorized job positions within the restaurant organization, including role title (e.g., Crew Member, Shift Lead, Kitchen Manager, FOH Supervisor), BOH/FOH classification, pay band, FLSA exemption status, required certifications (e.g., ServSafe), FTE equivalency factor, and whether the position is eligible for overtime. Serves as the job catalog for workforce planning and scheduling.'
}

Table workforce.shift {
  shift_id varchar [pk, note: 'Unique identifier for the shift record.']
  inspector_employee_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the user who scheduled the shift.']
  kitchen_station_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to restaurant.kitchen_station. Business justification: Shifts are assigned to specific kitchen stations (grill, prep, expo). The existing plain-text \'station\' column is a denormalization of kitchen_station. A proper FK enables station-level labor cost rep']
  position_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to workforce.position. Business justification: Each shift is worked in a specific authorized position (e.g., Crew Member, Shift Lead, BOH Cook). The shift table currently stores station (STRING) but has no FK to the canonical position record. Addi']
  primary_shift_employee_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the employee assigned to the shift.']
  unit_id varchar [note: 'Restaurant location where the shift occurs.']
  actual_end timestamp [note: 'Actual clock‑out timestamp recorded for the shift.']
  actual_hours decimal(5,2) [note: 'Actual worked hours for the shift (excluding breaks).']
  actual_start timestamp [note: 'Actual clock‑in timestamp recorded for the shift.']
  break_duration_minutes varchar [note: 'Total break time in minutes allocated for the shift.']
  code varchar [note: 'Human‑readable code for the shift (e.g., SHIFT20230915-001).']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the shift record was created.']
  daypart varchar [note: 'Part of the day the shift is scheduled for.']
  is_deleted boolean [note: 'Flag indicating soft deletion of the shift record.']
  labor_cost decimal(12,2) [note: 'Total labor cost for the shift (rate multiplied by actual hours).']
  labor_percentage decimal(5,2) [note: 'Labor cost expressed as a percentage of projected sales for the shift.']
  labor_rate_currency_code varchar [note: 'Three‑letter ISO currency code for the labor rate.']
  labor_rate_per_hour decimal(8,2) [note: 'Pay rate per hour for the employee on this shift.']
  notes varchar [note: 'Free‑form notes or comments about the shift.']
  on_call_flag boolean [note: 'Indicates if the shift is an on‑call assignment.']
  overtime_flag boolean [note: 'Indicates if the shift includes overtime hours.']
  scheduled_end timestamp [note: 'Planned end timestamp of the shift.']
  scheduled_hours decimal(5,2) [note: 'Planned shift duration in hours (excluding breaks).']
  scheduled_start timestamp [note: 'Planned start timestamp of the shift.']
  shift_date date [note: 'Calendar date of the shift (derived from scheduled_start).']
  shift_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle status of the shift.']
  shift_type varchar [note: 'Classification of the shift (e.g., regular, overtime, on‑call, training).']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent update to the shift record.']

  Note: 'Represents a scheduled work shift for an employee at a specific restaurant location, daypart (breakfast, lunch, dinner, late-night), and station assignment (grill, fry, drive-thru, expo, host, bar, dish). Captures planned start/end times, actual clock-in/clock-out times, assigned BOH/FOH station, shift type (regular, overtime, on-call, training), break duration, scheduling source (Planday), and swap/coverage details (original assignee, covering employee, swap request reason, swap approval status, approval timestamp) when shift reassignment occurs. Core operational record for labor deployment, station coverage planning, Speed of Service (SOS) staffing optimization, and Planday shift coverage workflows.'
}

Table workforce.schedule {
  schedule_id varchar [pk, note: 'Unique system-generated identifier for each labor schedule.']
  schedule_employee_id varchar [note: 'Unique identifier of the manager or supervisor who approved the schedule.']
  schedule_manager_employee_id varchar [note: 'Unique identifier of the manager or supervisor who approved the schedule.']
  schedule_restaurant_unit_id varchar [note: 'Unique identifier of the restaurant unit for which the schedule is created.']
  shift_id varchar [note: 'add column shift_id (BIGINT) with FK to workforce.shift.shift_id - schedules should reference the shifts they contain']
  approval_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Date‑time when the schedule was formally approved by the manager.']
  approved_by varchar [note: 'Display name of the manager who approved the schedule.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Date‑time when the schedule record was first created in the system.']
  daypart_schedule_notes varchar [note: 'Optional comments or special instructions for each daypart (e.g., event staffing, overtime alerts).']
  fte_evening decimal(5,2) [note: 'Planned full‑time equivalent staff for the evening shift (e.g., 16:00‑22:00).']
  fte_midday decimal(5,2) [note: 'Planned full‑time equivalent staff for the midday shift (e.g., 12:00‑16:00).']
  fte_morning decimal(5,2) [note: 'Planned full‑time equivalent staff for the morning shift (e.g., 06:00‑12:00).']
  fte_night decimal(5,2) [note: 'Planned full‑time equivalent staff for the night shift (e.g., 22:00‑02:00).']
  fte_total decimal(5,2) [note: 'Sum of planned FTEs across all dayparts for the schedule period.']
  labor_pct_evening decimal(5,2) [note: 'Target labor cost as a percentage of sales for the evening shift.']
  labor_pct_midday decimal(5,2) [note: 'Target labor cost as a percentage of sales for the midday shift.']
  labor_pct_morning decimal(5,2) [note: 'Target labor cost as a percentage of sales for the morning shift.']
  labor_pct_night decimal(5,2) [note: 'Target labor cost as a percentage of sales for the night shift.']
  labor_percentage decimal(5,2) [note: 'Target labor cost expressed as a percentage of projected sales for the period.']
  period_end_date date [note: 'Last calendar date covered by the schedule.']
  period_start_date date [note: 'First calendar date covered by the schedule.']
  schedule_number varchar [note: 'Human‑readable schedule code assigned by the scheduling system (e.g., SCHED‑2024‑W15).']
  schedule_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle state of the schedule: draft (editable), published (visible to staff), or locked (finalized).']
  total_scheduled_hours decimal(6,2) [note: 'Aggregate number of labor hours planned across all dayparts and positions for the schedule period.']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Date‑time of the most recent modification to the schedule.']
  version varchar [note: 'Incremental version of the schedule used for audit and rollback purposes.']

  Note: 'Weekly or period-level labor schedule published for a restaurant location, representing the planned staffing plan across all dayparts. Captures schedule period (start/end dates), restaurant unit, total scheduled hours, scheduled Labor%, FTE count by daypart, publication status (draft/published/locked), and the manager who approved the schedule. Links to individual shifts for granular staffing detail.'
}

Table workforce.time_entry {
  time_entry_id varchar [pk, note: 'Unique surrogate key for the time entry record.']
  payroll_record_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to workforce.payroll_record. Business justification: Time entries are the source data that payroll records aggregate. Linking time_entry.payroll_record_id to payroll_record creates a direct audit trail from individual clock-in/out events to the period p']
  primary_time_employee_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the employee who performed the time entry.']
  shift_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the scheduled shift associated with this time entry.']
  time_position_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to workforce.position. Business justification: Time entries capture actual clock-in/out events per role. The time_entry table stores job_role as a STRING, which duplicates the canonical position definition. Adding time_position_id normalizes this ']
  unit_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the restaurant location where the work was performed.']
  approved_by_manager boolean [note: 'True when a manager has approved the time entry.']
  approved_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Date‑time when the entry was approved by a manager.']
  break_flag boolean [note: 'True if a break was taken during the shift.']
  break_minutes varchar [note: 'Total minutes taken for breaks during the shift.']
  clock_in_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Exact date‑time when the employee clocked in.']
  clock_out_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Exact date‑time when the employee clocked out.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the time entry record was first created.']
  entry_number varchar [note: 'Human‑readable unique code for the time entry.']
  labor_cost decimal(12,2) [note: 'Monetary cost of the labor for this entry (total_hours * labor_rate).']
  labor_rate decimal(8,4) [note: 'Hourly wage rate applicable to the employee for this entry (in local currency).']
  missed_punch_flag boolean [note: 'True if the employee missed a clock‑in or clock‑out punch.']
  notes varchar [note: 'Optional free‑form comments or remarks about the time entry.']
  overtime_flag boolean [note: 'True if any overtime hours were recorded.']
  overtime_hours decimal(5,2) [note: 'Number of overtime hours worked.']
  pay_code varchar [note: 'Payroll code indicating the type of pay (regular, overtime, sick leave, etc.).']
  regular_hours decimal(5,2) [note: 'Number of non‑overtime hours worked.']
  scheduled_end_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Planned end date‑time of the shift according to the schedule.']
  scheduled_start_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Planned start date‑time of the shift according to the schedule.']
  time_entry_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle status of the time entry.']
  time_entry_type varchar [note: 'Method by which the time entry was recorded.']
  total_hours decimal(5,2) [note: 'Total number of hours (including overtime and breaks) recorded for the entry.']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent modification to the time entry.']
  work_date date [note: 'Calendar date on which the shift occurred.']

  Note: 'Captures actual clock-in and clock-out events for each employee per shift, sourced from Workday HCM time tracking or POS-integrated time clocks. Records regular hours, overtime hours, break time, missed punch flags, and manager approval status. Foundation for payroll processing, Labor% calculation, and compliance with FLSA/OSHA labor regulations.'
}

Table workforce.payroll_record {
  payroll_record_id varchar [pk, note: 'System-generated unique identifier for the payroll record.']
  department_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the internal department (e.g., FOH, BOH) for cost allocation.']
  employee_id varchar [note: 'Unique identifier of the employee to whom the payroll pertains.']
  unit_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the restaurant/location where the employee works.']
  benefit_deduction decimal(12,2) [note: 'Sum of employee benefit contributions deducted.']
  bonus_amount decimal(12,2) [note: 'Monetary value of any bonus paid.']
  commission_amount decimal(12,2) [note: 'Monetary value of commission earned.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the payroll record was first created.']
  currency_code varchar [note: 'ISO 4217 currency code for monetary amounts.']
  employee_address varchar [note: 'Mailing address of the employee.']
  employee_type varchar [note: 'Classification of employment relationship.']
  fiscal_period varchar [note: 'Period number within the fiscal year (e.g., Q1, Q2).']
  gross_pay decimal(12,2) [note: 'Total earnings before any deductions.']
  is_bonus boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the payroll includes a bonus component.']
  job_title varchar [note: 'Official job title or position held by the employee.']
  labor_percent decimal(5,2) [note: 'Labor cost as a percentage of sales for the period.']
  net_pay decimal(12,2) [note: 'Take‑home earnings after all deductions.']
  notes varchar [note: 'Free‑form comments or remarks about the payroll record.']
  overtime_hours decimal(6,2) [note: 'Total overtime hours worked.']
  overtime_rate decimal(8,2) [note: 'Hourly rate applied to overtime hours.']
  pay_date date [note: 'Date on which the net amount is disbursed to the employee.']
  pay_period_end date [note: 'Last calendar day of the payroll period.']
  pay_period_start date [note: 'First calendar day of the payroll period.']
  pay_rate decimal(8,2) [note: 'Standard hourly or salary rate for the employee.']
  payroll_date timestamp [note: 'Exact time the payroll was run in the source system.']
  payroll_number varchar [note: 'Human‑readable payroll reference number assigned by the payroll system.']
  payroll_record_status varchar [note: 'Current processing state of the payroll record.']
  payroll_type varchar [note: 'Nature of compensation for the period.']
  regular_hours decimal(6,2) [note: 'Total non‑overtime hours worked.']
  tax_withheld decimal(12,2) [note: 'Total tax amount deducted from gross pay.']
  tax_year varchar [note: 'Fiscal year applicable for tax reporting.']
  tip_amount decimal(12,2) [note: 'Tips declared by the employee for the period.']
  union_member_flag boolean [note: 'True if the employee is a union member.']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent modification to the payroll record.']

  Note: 'Period-level payroll summary for each employee, capturing gross pay, net pay, regular hours paid, overtime hours paid, tips declared, deductions (benefits, taxes, garnishments), pay period dates, and payroll run status. Sourced from Workday HCM payroll module. Serves as the authoritative payroll transaction record for finance integration and Labor% reporting.'
}

Table workforce.certification {
  certification_id varchar [pk, note: 'System-generated unique identifier for the certification record.']
  brand_standard_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to restaurant.brand_standard. Business justification: Brand standards mandate specific certifications (ServSafe, food handler, allergen training). Linking certification to the requiring brand_standard enables compliance gap analysis — identifying employe']
  employee_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the employee who holds this certification.']
  required_for_position_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to workforce.position. Business justification: Certifications (ServSafe, food handler cards, allergen training) are required for specific authorized positions. The certification table stores related_role as a STRING, which is a denormalized refere']
  restaurant_unit_id varchar [note: 'add column restaurant_unit_id (BIGINT) with FK to restaurant.unit.unit_id - certifications should be trackable at the unit level for compliance reporting']
  certification_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle status of the certification.']
  certification_type varchar [note: 'Category of certification required for foodservice operations.']
  code varchar [note: 'Business identifier or code assigned to the certification by the issuing body.']
  compliance_status varchar [note: 'Regulatory compliance assessment of the certification.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the certification record was first created in the system.']
  document_url varchar [note: 'Link to the scanned certification document stored in the document repository.']
  expiration_date date [note: 'Date the certification expires and must be renewed.']
  is_mandatory boolean [note: 'Indicates if the certification is required for the employee\'s role.']
  issue_date date [note: 'Date the certification was originally issued.']
  issuing_body varchar [note: 'Organization that issued the certification.']
  last_verified_date date [note: 'Date when the certification was last validated by internal audit.']
  name varchar [note: 'Human‑readable name of the certification (e.g., ServSafe Food Handler).']
  notes varchar [note: 'Free‑form notes regarding the certification (e.g., special conditions, comments).']
  renewal_notice_date date [note: 'Date a renewal reminder is sent to the employee.']
  renewal_required boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the certification must be renewed before expiration.']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent update to the certification record.']

  Note: 'Single source of truth for all employee learning credentials, training completions, and regulatory certifications required for foodservice operations. Covers external certifications (ServSafe Food Handler, ServSafe Manager, allergen awareness, HACCP, alcohol service permits, OSHA safety) and internal training completions (new hire orientation, food safety modules, POS/KDS operation, BOH/FOH SOPs, LTO product training, management development). Captures credential type, issuing body (NRA ServSafe, local health department, internal L&D), delivery method (in-person, e-learning, OJT), issue/completion date, expiration date, assessment score, trainer/facilitator, and compliance status. Critical for food safety regulatory compliance, health department inspections, scheduling eligibility validation, and employee development tracking.'
}

Table workforce.training_completion {
  training_completion_id varchar [pk, note: 'System-generated unique identifier for each training completion record.']
  earned_certification_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to workforce.certification. Business justification: Completing a training program often results in earning a certification (e.g., completing ServSafe training → ServSafe certification). The training_completion table has certification_required (BOOLEAN)']
  haccp_plan_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to foodsafety.haccp_plan. Business justification: Regulatory compliance requires employees to be trained on the specific HACCP plan version in effect. This FK links training completions to the exact HACCP plan covered, enabling plan-version-specific ']
  primary_training_employee_id varchar [note: 'Unique identifier of the employee who completed the training.']
  recipe_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to menu.recipe. Business justification: New menu item rollout requires BOH staff training on specific recipes (prep method, cook temps, HACCP CCPs). Linking training_completion to recipe enables tracking which employees are certified on eac']
  shift_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the work shift associated with the training session.']
  unit_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the restaurant location where the training was completed.']
  assessment_max_score decimal(5,2) [note: 'Maximum possible score for the assessment.']
  assessment_passed boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the employee passed the assessment (true) or not (false).']
  assessment_score decimal(5,2) [note: 'Score achieved by the employee on the training assessment.']
  certification_expiration_date date [note: 'Date when the certification earned from this training expires, if applicable.']
  certification_required boolean [note: 'True if the training results in a required certification for the employee.']
  completion_number varchar [note: 'Business-assigned code for the training completion event.']
  completion_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Date and time when the employee finished the training.']
  compliance_status varchar [note: 'Indicates whether the training satisfies regulatory or internal compliance requirements.']

  trainer_name varchar [note: 'Full name of the trainer or facilitator.']

  training_type varchar [note: 'Broad classification of the training content.']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent update to the training completion record.']

  Note: 'Records completion of training programs by employees, including new hire orientation, food safety modules, POS operation, KDS usage, BOH/FOH SOPs, LTO product training, and management development programs. Captures training program name, delivery method (in-person, e-learning, OJT), completion date, assessment score, and trainer/facilitator. Supports compliance tracking and performance development.'
}

  Note: 'Master record for each restaurant units Hazard Analysis and Critical Control Points (HACCP) plan, including plan version, scope, approval status, regulatory framework alignment (FDA FSMA, ISO 22000, Codex Alimentarius), effective and expiration dates, responsible food safety manager, team members, and prerequisite program references. Serves as the authoritative SSOT for HACCP program governance across all company-owned and franchised units. Each plan undergoes annual review and revalidation.'
}

Table foodsafety.critical_control_point {
  critical_control_point_id varchar [pk, note: 'Unique system-generated identifier for the critical control point.']
  haccp_plan_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to foodsafety.haccp_plan. Business justification: Each Critical Control Point belongs to a single HACCP plan; adding haccp_plan_id creates the required parent link.']
  kitchen_station_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to restaurant.kitchen_station. Business justification: Each CCP maps to a specific kitchen station where the control point is monitored (e.g., fry station CCP for oil temperature, receiving dock CCP for incoming ingredient temps). HACCP plans assign CCPs ']
  responsible_employee_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to workforce.employee. Business justification: Required: Each CCP must have a qualified employee monitor it; linking enables CCP compliance reports and corrective‑action tracking.']
  unit_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to restaurant.unit. Business justification: CCPs are defined and monitored at specific restaurant units. Operations and food safety managers query all active CCPs for a given unit for health inspection preparation and HACCP compliance reporting']
  average_deviation_value decimal(10,2) [note: 'Average magnitude of deviations observed across monitoring events.']
  code varchar [note: 'Business identifier code used to reference the CCP in SOPs and audits.']
  corrective_action_procedure varchar [note: 'Standardized steps to take when a deviation from the critical limit occurs.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the CCP record was first created in the system.']
  critical_control_point_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle status of the CCP.']
  critical_limit_max decimal(10,2) [note: 'Upper bound of the acceptable range for the control parameter (e.g., maximum temperature).']
  critical_limit_min decimal(10,2) [note: 'Lower bound of the acceptable range for the control parameter (e.g., minimum temperature).']
  deviation_count varchar [note: 'Cumulative number of recorded deviations from the critical limits.']
  effective_end_date date [note: 'Date when the CCP is retired or superseded; null if still active.']
  effective_start_date date [note: 'Date when the CCP became effective.']
  haccp_plan_version varchar [note: 'Version identifier of the HACCP plan to which this CCP belongs.']
  hazard_type varchar [note: 'Category of hazard the CCP is designed to control.']
  is_critical boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the CCP is classified as a critical control point (true) or a control point (false).']
  last_monitored_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent monitoring observation.']
  last_verification_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent verification activity.']
  monitoring_frequency varchar [note: 'How often the CCP is monitored.']
  monitoring_method varchar [note: 'Technique or instrument used to monitor the CCP (e.g., calibrated thermometer).']
  name varchar [note: 'Human‑readable name of the critical control point.']
  notes varchar [note: 'Free‑form comments or observations related to the CCP.']
  process_step varchar [note: 'Operational step in the food preparation flow where the CCP applies.']
  regulatory_reference varchar [note: 'Applicable regulatory or certification reference (e.g., FDA FSMA, ISO 22000, ServSafe).']
  responsible_role varchar [note: 'Job role accountable for monitoring and maintaining the CCP.']
  unit_of_measure varchar [note: 'Measurement unit for the critical limit values.']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent update to the CCP record.']
  verification_frequency varchar [note: 'How often the CCP is independently verified for compliance.']
  verification_method varchar [note: 'Method used during verification (e.g., internal audit, third‑party audit).']

  Note: 'Defines each Critical Control Point (CCP) within a HACCP plan, including the hazard type (biological, chemical, physical), critical limits (min/max temperature, pH, time), monitoring method, corrective action procedure, and verification frequency. Each CCP is tied to a specific process step (e.g., cooking, cooling, receiving) and HACCP plan version.'
}

Table foodsafety.temperature_log {

Replacing this with a proper FK food_safety_audit_id enables structured joins f']
  kitchen_station_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to restaurant.kitchen_station. Business justification: Temperature monitoring is performed at specific kitchen stations (walk-in cooler, hot-hold station, receiving dock). Station-level temperature compliance is a core HACCP CCP monitoring requirement. Fo']

  shift_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the workforce shift during which the reading was taken.']
  stock_location_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the restaurant location or specific area (e.g., kitchen, dock) where the reading occurred.']
  temperature_equipment_asset_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the equipment or sensor that generated the temperature reading.']
  unit_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to restaurant.unit. Business justification: Temperature logs are recorded at a specific restaurant unit. Unit-level temperature compliance reporting, health inspection submissions, and regulatory filings require a direct unit FK without joining']
  batch_number varchar [note: 'Optional batch identifier grouping a set of readings for a specific audit period.']

  critical_limit_high decimal(6,2) [note: 'Upper temperature threshold defined by HACCP for the monitoring point.']
  critical_limit_low decimal(6,2) [note: 'Lower temperature threshold defined by HACCP for the monitoring point.']
  data_quality_flag boolean [note: 'Indicator of the data quality assessment for the reading.']
  deviation_flag boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the temperature reading falls outside the defined critical limits.']
  maintenance_due_date date [note: 'Planned date for required maintenance activities.']
  maintenance_required boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the equipment requires maintenance based on the reading or schedule.']

  temperature_trend varchar [note: 'Observed trend of temperature change relative to previous readings.']
  temperature_value decimal(6,2) [note: 'Measured temperature value captured by the sensor or manual probe.']
  unit_of_measure varchar [note: 'Unit of measure for the temperature reading (Fahrenheit or Celsius).']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent update to the temperature log record.']

  Note: 'Time-series log of temperature readings captured at critical monitoring points (walk-in coolers, freezers, hot-holding units, cooking equipment, receiving docks), including equipment ID, reading timestamp, measured temperature, unit of measure (°F/°C), critical limit thresholds, deviation flag, and monitoring method (manual probe, automated sensor). Core HACCP monitoring record per Principle 4.'
}

Table foodsafety.sanitation_schedule {
  sanitation_schedule_id varchar [pk, note: 'Unique identifier for the sanitation schedule.']
  department_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to workforce.department. Business justification: Sanitation schedules are operationally assigned to specific restaurant departments (kitchen, dishwashing, bar). This FK enables department managers to view their cleaning obligations, supports labor p']
  equipment_asset_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to restaurant.equipment_asset. Business justification: Sanitation schedules are written for specific equipment assets (e.g., \'sanitize the slicer after each use\', \'clean the fryer every 4 hours\'). The plain-text \'equipment\' field is a denormalization of e']
  haccp_plan_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to foodsafety.haccp_plan. Business justification: Sanitation schedules (Master Sanitation Schedules) are defined as part of the HACCP plan framework — each sanitation task supports the hazard controls identified in the HACCP plan. haccp_plan already ']
  kitchen_station_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to restaurant.kitchen_station. Business justification: Sanitation schedules in restaurants are written at the kitchen station level (e.g., fryer station cleaned every 4 hours). Food safety managers assign sanitation tasks to specific stations. The plain-t']
  responsible_employee_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to workforce.employee. Business justification: Required: Sanitation schedule assigns a specific employee; FK provides traceability for health inspections and internal audits.']
  stock_location_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to inventory.stock_location. Business justification: Sanitation schedules are defined per storage area; the FK associates each schedule with its location.']
  unit_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to restaurant.unit. Business justification: Sanitation schedules are assigned to specific restaurant units. Operations managers schedule and track cleaning tasks per unit; health inspectors verify sanitation compliance per unit during inspectio']
  allergen_control_flag boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the task includes allergen control measures.']
  audit_required_flag boolean [note: 'Whether a post‑task audit is required.']
  chemical_concentration varchar [note: 'Required concentration of the chemical (e.g., "200 ppm").']
  chemical_name varchar [note: 'Name of the chemical or sanitizer used.']
  cleaning_method varchar [note: 'Method used to perform the cleaning (e.g., manual, automated, steam).']
  compliance_status varchar [note: 'Current compliance status of the task against food‑safety regulations.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the schedule record was created.']
  description varchar [note: 'Detailed description of the schedule purpose and scope.']
  effective_from date [note: 'Date when the schedule becomes effective.']
  effective_until date [note: 'Date when the schedule expires or is superseded (nullable).']
  frequency varchar [note: 'Number of times the task occurs within the defined frequency unit.']
  frequency_unit varchar [note: 'Time unit for the task frequency.']
  is_mandatory boolean [note: 'Indicates if the task is mandatory for compliance.']
  last_performed_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent execution of the task.']
  next_due_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Scheduled timestamp for the next required execution of the task.']
  notes varchar [note: 'Free‑form field for any supplemental information or comments.']
  priority_level varchar [note: 'Priority assigned to the task for scheduling and resource allocation.']
  responsible_role varchar [note: 'Job role responsible for executing the task (e.g., Shift Lead, Kitchen Manager).']
  sanitation_schedule_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle status of the schedule.']
  schedule_code varchar [note: 'Business code used to reference the schedule.']
  schedule_name varchar [note: 'Human‑readable name of the schedule.']
  sop_reference varchar [note: 'Identifier of the Standard Operating Procedure that governs the task.']
  task_name varchar [note: 'Name of the specific cleaning or sanitizing task.']
  temperature_requirement_celsius decimal(5,2) [note: 'Required temperature condition for the cleaning task, expressed in Celsius.']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent update to the schedule record.']
  version_number varchar [note: 'Version number of the schedule for change management.']
  waste_disposal_method varchar [note: 'Method used to dispose of waste generated by the task (e.g., biohazard, recyclable).']

  Note: 'Master sanitation schedule (MSS) with execution log for each restaurant unit, including schedule template (task name, target area: FOH/BOH zone/equipment, frequency: hourly/daily/weekly, chemical/sanitizer, concentration requirements, responsible role, SOP reference) and task execution records (completion timestamp, employee, actual concentration measured, pass/fail, deviation notes). Managed via Zenput task management. Serves as both the authoritative sanitation template and the compliance evidence of task completion.'
}

Table foodsafety.allergen_incident {
  allergen_incident_id varchar [pk, note: 'System-generated unique identifier for the allergen incident record.']
  allergen_restaurant_unit_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the restaurant location where the incident occurred.']
  employee_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the staff member who responded to or recorded the incident.']
  guest_order_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the POS order associated with the incident, if applicable.']
  ingredient_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to supply.ingredient. Business justification: Needed for root‑cause analysis: trace allergen incidents to the specific ingredient causing the reaction, supporting recall and corrective action reports.']
  ingredient_lot_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to supply.ingredient_lot. Business justification: When an allergen incident involves a specific lot, linking to lot tracking provides precise traceability for recalls.']
  order_item_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to order.order_item. Business justification: FDA MedWatch filings and allergen root cause analysis require identifying the specific order line item (not just the guest order) that triggered the reaction. allergen_incident already links to guest_']
  primary_allergen_guest_profile_id varchar [note: 'Unique identifier of the guest who reported the allergen reaction.']
  recipe_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to menu.recipe. Business justification: Allergen incident investigations require tracing the exact recipe version prepared at the time of the incident to identify whether a recipe change, substitution, or prep error caused the exposure. Exi']
  shift_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the employee shift during which the incident happened.']
  stock_item_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to inventory.stock_item. Business justification: Allergen incidents are traced to the offending ingredient; the FK connects the incident to the stock item for root‑cause analysis.']
  temperature_log_id varchar [note: 'Reference to the temperature monitoring record associated with the incident, if relevant.']
  visit_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to guest.guest_visit. Business justification: Allergen incident root-cause analysis requires knowing the exact visit (daypart, server, table) during which the incident occurred. Linking allergen_incident to guest_visit enables operational reports']
  allergen_code varchar [note: 'Standardized code for the allergen (e.g., ISO 22000 allergen code).']
  allergen_incident_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle state of the incident.']
  allergen_name varchar [note: 'Name of the allergen involved in the incident (e.g., peanuts, shellfish).']
  complaint_description varchar [note: 'Narrative provided by the guest describing the allergic reaction and circumstances.']
  compliance_flag boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the incident complies with internal SOPs and external regulations.']
  corrective_action varchar [note: 'Planned or executed corrective measures to prevent recurrence.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the incident record was first created in the system.']
  fda_medwatch_filed boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the incident was reported to the FDA MedWatch system.']
  guest_contact_info varchar [note: 'Phone number or email address used to reach the guest.']
  guest_contact_method varchar [note: 'Preferred method used to contact the guest for follow‑up.']
  immediate_action_taken varchar [note: 'Actions performed at the time of the incident (e.g., administered epinephrine, called emergency services).']
  incident_category varchar [note: 'High‑level classification of the incident type.']
  incident_location varchar [note: 'Physical location within the restaurant where the incident occurred (e.g., kitchen, dining area).']
  incident_notes varchar [note: 'Free‑form notes captured by staff during investigation.']
  incident_number varchar [note: 'Business-facing identifier assigned to the incident for tracking and reporting.']
  incident_resolution_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Date and time when the incident was formally closed or resolved.']
  incident_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Date and time when the allergen incident was observed or reported.']
  investigation_complete boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the root‑cause investigation has been completed.']
  investigation_complete_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Date and time when the investigation was marked complete.']
  is_repeat_incident boolean [note: 'Indicates whether a similar allergen incident has been recorded previously at the same location.']
  notification_date date [note: 'Date on which the regulatory notification was submitted.']
  regulatory_notification_status varchar [note: 'Status of required notifications to regulatory bodies (e.g., FDA MedWatch).']
  reported_by varchar [note: 'Name or identifier of the staff member who logged the incident.']
  root_cause varchar [note: 'Identified underlying cause of the allergen exposure (e.g., cross‑contamination, mislabeling).']
  severity_score varchar [note: 'Numeric representation of incident severity (e.g., 1‑5 scale) for analytics.']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent modification to the incident record.']

  Note: 'Transactional record of a reported allergen-related incident at a restaurant unit, including incident date, guest complaint details, allergen involved, menu item implicated, severity (mild reaction, anaphylaxis, hospitalization), immediate response actions taken, root cause determination, and regulatory notification status (FDA MedWatch if applicable).'
}

Table foodsafety.illness_report {
  illness_report_id varchar [pk, note: 'Unique identifier for the illness report record.']
  employee_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the employee who reported or is associated with the illness.']
  ingredient_lot_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to supply.ingredient_lot. Business justification: Foodborne illness outbreak investigations require direct traceability from the illness report to the specific ingredient lot suspected as the source. FSMA traceability rules and FDA outbreak response ']
  member_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to loyalty.member. Business justification: When a loyalty member reports a foodborne illness after dining, the illness report must be linked to their member profile for regulatory notification tracking, targeted guest outreach, and potential g']
  menu_item_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to menu.menu_item. Business justification: Foodborne illness investigations require linking the report to the specific menu item suspected as the vehicle of illness for health department notification, menu item withdrawal decisions, and outbre']
  primary_illness_restaurant_unit_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the restaurant location where the incident occurred.']
  shift_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the work shift during which the employee fell ill.']
  suspected_order_item_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to order.order_item. Business justification: Outbreak investigation and health department regulatory reporting require identifying the exact order line item that caused illness. \'suspected_food_item\' is a denormalized text field replaced by this']
  action_plan varchar [note: 'Planned actions to prevent recurrence.']
  action_plan_completed_date date [note: 'Date when the action plan was completed.']
  action_plan_due_date date [note: 'Target date for completing the action plan.']
  compliance_reference varchar [note: 'Reference to the specific regulatory requirement or guidance applicable to the report.']
  corrective_action_taken varchar [note: 'Description of any corrective action performed in response to the incident.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the report record was first created in the system.']
  exclusion_decision boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the employee was excluded from work due to the illness.']
  exclusion_start_date date [note: 'Date when the employee\'s work exclusion began.']
  health_department_notification_date date [note: 'Date when the health department was notified about the incident.']
  health_department_notified boolean [note: 'Flag indicating whether the local health department was notified.']
  illness_report_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle status of the illness report.']
  investigation_end_date date [note: 'Date when the health investigation concluded.']
  investigation_start_date date [note: 'Date when the health investigation began.']
  investigation_status varchar [note: 'Current status of the health investigation.']
  notes varchar [note: 'Free‑form notes captured by investigators or managers.']
  onset_date date [note: 'Date when the employee first experienced symptoms.']
  report_method varchar [note: 'Method by which the illness was reported.']
  report_number varchar [note: 'Business identifier assigned to the illness report.']
  report_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Date and time when the illness was reported.']
  return_to_work_date date [note: 'Date when the employee was cleared to return to work.']
  root_cause varchar [note: 'Identified root cause of the illness, if determined.']
  severity_level varchar [note: 'Categorical severity level derived from the severity score.']
  severity_score varchar [note: 'Numeric score representing the severity of the reported illness.']
  suspected_pathogen varchar [note: 'Pathogen suspected to have caused the illness, if known.']
  symptoms varchar [note: 'Symptoms reported by the employee, captured for health analysis.']
  updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp of the most recent update to the report record.']

  Note: 'Transactional record of a reported employee foodborne illness or suspected foodborne illness event at a restaurant unit, capturing report date, employee role, symptoms reported, onset date, suspected pathogen or food item, exclusion-from-work decision, return-to-work clearance date, and health department notification status. Supports FDA Food Code employee health policy compliance.'
}

Table foodsafety.audit_ccp_finding {
  audit_ccp_finding_id varchar [pk, note: 'Primary key for the audit_ccp_finding association']
  critical_control_point_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to the Critical Control Point that was evaluated and produced this finding.']
  food_safety_audit_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to the food safety audit during which this CCP finding was recorded.']
  compliance_status varchar [note: 'Compliance classification for this CCP as assessed during this specific audit. Cannot reside on the CCP (which defines the standard) or the audit header (which aggregates all CCPs).']
  corrective_action_required varchar [note: 'Specific corrective action mandated for this CCP finding within this audit. Distinct from the CCP\'s generic corrective_action_procedure, this is the instance-level action for this specific deviation event.']
  deviation_noted boolean [note: 'Indicates whether a deviation from the critical limit was observed for this CCP during this audit. Drives corrective action workflows.']
  evaluated_at timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when this specific CCP was evaluated during the audit. Supports sequencing of findings within a multi-CCP audit.']
  finding_result varchar [note: 'The outcome of evaluating this specific CCP during the audit — whether it passed, failed, or a deviation was observed. Belongs to the finding, not to the audit aggregate or the CCP definition.']
  finding_score decimal [note: 'Numeric score assigned to this CCP\'s performance within this audit. Contributes to the audit\'s overall compliance_score but is specific to this CCP-audit intersection.']
  finding_status varchar [note: 'Lifecycle status of this individual CCP finding — tracks whether the corrective action has been addressed and the finding closed.']

  Note: 'This association product represents the Event between food_safety_audit and critical_control_point. It captures the individual finding recorded for each Critical Control Point evaluated during a specific food safety audit. Each record links one food_safety_audit to one critical_control_point and carries the outcome data — finding result, compliance status, deviation details, corrective action required, and finding score — that exists only in the context of evaluating a specific CCP within a specific audit. Food safety managers actively create, review, and close these records as part of the audit lifecycle.. Existence Justification: In HACCP-governed food safety operations, each audit systematically evaluates multiple Critical Control Points, and each CCP is evaluated across many audits over its lifecycle. The intersection — an \'audit CCP finding\' — is a recognized operational record that auditors actively create during each audit, capturing whether each CCP passed or failed, any deviation observed, and what corrective action is required. This is not derivable from existing FKs; it is a first-class operational entity that food safety managers create, review, and act upon.'
}

Table loyalty.member {
  member_id varchar [pk, note: 'Unique identifier for the loyalty program member. Primary key for the member entity.']
  account_manager_employee_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to workforce.employee. Business justification: Needed for assigning a dedicated employee to manage high‑value members, enabling personalized service and performance reporting.']
  current_tier_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to loyalty.tier. Business justification: member.current_tier is currently a STRING denormalization of the tier name. Adding current_tier_id as a FK to loyalty.tier.tier_id normalizes this relationship, enabling proper JOIN-based tier lookups']
  enrollment_channel_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to order.channel. Business justification: Member enrollment channel attribution is critical for loyalty program ROI analysis by channel and digital acquisition reporting. enrollment_channel is currently a plain text denormalization of the cha']
  member_guest_profile_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key reference to the guest domain master record. Links loyalty membership to the guest profile system of record.']
  member_preferred_location_unit_id varchar [note: 'Restaurant location that the member visits most frequently or has designated as their preferred location. Used for personalized offers and location-based campaigns.']
  member_profile_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key reference to the guest domain master record. Links loyalty membership to the guest profile system of record.']
  member_unit_id varchar [note: 'Restaurant location where the member enrolled, if enrollment occurred at a physical location. Null for digital enrollments.']
  primary_member_preferred_location_unit_id varchar [note: 'Restaurant location that the member visits most frequently or has designated as their preferred location. Used for personalized offers and location-based campaigns.']
  program_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to loyalty.program. Business justification: Supports franchisee‑level loyalty analytics by linking each member to the owning franchisee, used in franchise performance dashboards.']
  referred_by_member_id varchar [note: 'Member ID of the existing member who referred this member to the loyalty program. Null if member enrolled without a referral.']
  account_closure_date date [note: 'Date when the loyalty membership was closed or terminated. Null for active memberships. Used for retention analysis and right-to-be-forgotten compliance.']
  account_closure_reason varchar [note: 'Business reason for account closure. Examples: member request, fraud, inactivity, duplicate account, policy violation, data privacy request.']
  account_created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the loyalty member record was first created in the system. Audit field for data lineage and compliance.']
  account_updated_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when the loyalty member record was last modified. Audit field for data lineage and change tracking.']
  badges_earned varchar [note: 'Total number of achievement badges earned by the member through gamification activities, challenges, and milestones.']
  birthday_month varchar [note: 'Month of the member\'s birthday (1-12). Stored without year or day for privacy. Used for birthday reward campaigns and personalized offers.']
  current_points_balance varchar [note: 'Current available points balance that the member can redeem. Calculated as lifetime earned minus lifetime redeemed minus expired points.']
  data_privacy_consent_date date [note: 'Date when the member provided explicit consent for data collection, processing, and storage under applicable privacy regulations.']
  direct_mail_opt_in boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the member has consented to receive physical direct mail offers and communications. True if opted in, False if opted out.']
  email_opt_in boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the member has consented to receive promotional and transactional emails. True if opted in, False if opted out.']
  enrollment_date date [note: 'Date when the guest enrolled in the loyalty program. Marks the start of the member lifecycle.']
  gamification_level varchar [note: 'Current level in the loyalty program gamification system. Members progress through levels by completing challenges, earning badges, and accumulating points.']
  last_activity_date date [note: 'Date of the most recent loyalty program activity by the member, including points earned, points redeemed, or profile updates. Used for churn prediction and reactivation campaigns.']
  last_transaction_date date [note: 'Date of the most recent purchase transaction where the member earned or redeemed points. Subset of last_activity_date focused on revenue-generating activity.']
  lifetime_points_earned varchar [note: 'Total cumulative loyalty points earned by the member since enrollment. Never decreases; used for tier qualification and lifetime value analysis.']
  lifetime_points_redeemed varchar [note: 'Total cumulative loyalty points redeemed by the member for rewards, discounts, or offers since enrollment.']
  membership_number varchar [note: 'Externally-facing unique membership identifier displayed on loyalty cards, mobile app, and customer communications. Human-readable alphanumeric code.']
  next_expiration_date date [note: 'Date when the next batch of points will expire. Null if no points are scheduled to expire.']
  nps_score varchar [note: 'Most recent Net Promoter Score provided by the member, ranging from 0 (detractor) to 10 (promoter). Measures member satisfaction and likelihood to recommend.']
  nps_survey_date date [note: 'Date when the most recent NPS survey was completed by the member.']
  points_expiring_soon varchar [note: 'Number of points scheduled to expire within the next 30 days. Used for proactive member engagement and retention campaigns.']
  preferred_language varchar [note: 'Member\'s preferred language for communications and app interface. Three-letter ISO 639-2 language code.']
  program_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle status of the loyalty membership. Active: member in good standing. Suspended: temporarily blocked due to policy violation. Churned: member opted out or account closed. Inactive: no activity for extended period. Pending: enrollment initiated but not completed.']
  push_notification_opt_in boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the member has consented to receive push notifications through the mobile app. True if opted in, False if opted out.']
  referral_code varchar [note: 'Unique referral code assigned to this member for member-get-member campaigns. When shared and used by new enrollees, both parties receive bonus points.']
  sms_opt_in boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the member has consented to receive promotional and transactional SMS text messages. True if opted in, False if opted out.']
  status_reason varchar [note: 'Business reason or explanation for the current program status. Examples: fraud detection, member request, inactivity, policy violation, system migration.']
  terms_accepted_date date [note: 'Date when the member accepted the loyalty program terms and conditions. Required for legal compliance and dispute resolution.']
  terms_version varchar [note: 'Version number of the loyalty program terms and conditions that the member accepted. Format: v1.0, v2.1, etc.']
  third_party_sharing_opt_in boolean [note: 'Indicates whether the member has consented to sharing their data with third-party partners for marketing purposes. True if opted in, False if opted out.']
  tier_effective_date date [note: 'Date when the member achieved or was assigned their current tier level. Used for tier anniversary calculations and renewal cycles.']
  tier_expiration_date date [note: 'Date when the current tier status expires and member will be re-evaluated for tier qualification. Null for lifetime tiers.']
  total_visits varchar [note: 'Total number of restaurant visits or orders placed by the member since enrollment. Includes dine-in, drive-thru, OLO, and 3PD orders where loyalty was applied.']

  Note: 'Master record for every enrolled loyalty program member. Captures member identity linkage (guest_id FK to guest domain), enrollment channel (OLO, POS, in-app, kiosk, staff-assisted), enrollment date, current tier assignment, lifetime points earned/redeemed, opt-in preferences, program assignment, auto-accrue payment linkage flag, and program status (active, suspended, churned). This is the SSOT for loyalty membership identity — distinct from the guest profile master in the guest domain.'
}

Table loyalty.tier {
  tier_id varchar [pk, note: 'Unique identifier for the loyalty membership tier. Primary key.']
  program_id varchar [note: 'add column program_id (BIGINT) with FK to loyalty.program.program_id - tiers are meaningless without association to a loyalty program']
  annual_fee_amount decimal(10,2) [note: 'Annual membership fee charged to maintain this tier status, if applicable. Null or zero indicates no fee. Used for premium tier monetization.']
  benefits_summary varchar [note: 'Comma-separated or structured text summary of key benefits and perks associated with this tier (e.g., free delivery, birthday rewards, exclusive offers, priority support). Used for member communications and benefit entitlement logic.']
  birthday_reward_eligible boolean [note: 'Indicates whether members in this tier receive a special birthday reward (e.g., free item, bonus points, discount). True if eligible, false otherwise.']
  code varchar [note: 'Short alphanumeric code uniquely identifying the tier (e.g., BRONZE, SILVER, GOLD, PLATINUM). Used as business identifier in operational systems and reporting.']
  color_code varchar [note: 'Hexadecimal color code used for tier branding in digital channels (e.g., #CD7F32 for bronze, #C0C0C0 for silver, #FFD700 for gold). Used in mobile app, website, and marketing materials.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when this tier record was first created in the loyalty system. Used for audit trail and data lineage.']
  description varchar [note: 'Detailed description of the tier, including value proposition, benefits summary, and marketing messaging. Used in loyalty program communications and member portal.']
  downgrade_threshold decimal(12,2) [note: 'Minimum threshold value required to maintain this tier status during re-evaluation period. Falling below this value triggers downgrade to lower tier. Null indicates no downgrade policy.']
  early_access_lto boolean [note: 'Indicates whether members in this tier receive early access to new Limited Time Offers (LTO) before general availability. True if eligible, false otherwise.']
  effective_end_date date [note: 'Date when this tier configuration was retired or replaced. Null indicates the tier is currently active. Used for tier lifecycle management and historical analysis.']
  effective_start_date date [note: 'Date when this tier configuration became active and available for member assignment. Used for tier lifecycle management and historical analysis.']
  exclusive_offers_eligible boolean [note: 'Indicates whether members in this tier receive access to exclusive promotional offers and Limited Time Offers (LTO) not available to lower tiers. True if eligible, false otherwise.']
  free_delivery_eligible boolean [note: 'Indicates whether members in this tier are eligible for free delivery on Online Ordering (OLO) and Third-Party Delivery (3PD) orders. True if eligible, false otherwise.']
  grace_period_days varchar [note: 'Number of days after tier expiration during which members retain tier benefits before downgrade. Used to provide buffer period for re-qualification.']
  icon_url varchar [note: 'URL path to the tier badge or icon image asset used in member portal, mobile app, and digital communications. Supports tier visual identity and gamification.']
  last_modified_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when this tier record was last updated. Used for audit trail, change tracking, and data synchronization.']
  launch_date date [note: 'Date when this tier was officially launched to the market and communicated to guests. May differ from effective_start_date for soft launches or pilot programs.']
  max_redemption_discount_pct decimal(5,2) [note: 'Maximum percentage discount that can be applied when redeeming points for rewards in this tier (e.g., 20.00 for 20% max discount). Used to control reward economics and prevent abuse.']
  modified_by_user varchar [note: 'Username or identifier of the user who last modified this tier configuration. Used for audit trail and accountability.']
  name varchar [note: 'Human-readable display name of the loyalty tier (e.g., Bronze Member, Silver Member, Gold Member, Platinum Elite). Used in guest-facing communications and marketing materials.']
  points_multiplier decimal(5,2) [note: 'Multiplier applied to base points accrual for members in this tier (e.g., 1.0 for base, 1.5 for 50% bonus, 2.0 for double points). Used to calculate accelerated points earning.']
  priority_support_eligible boolean [note: 'Indicates whether members in this tier receive priority customer support (e.g., dedicated phone line, faster response times). True if eligible, false otherwise.']
  qualification_metric varchar [note: 'The metric used to determine tier qualification (points accrued, visit count, total spend amount, or transaction count). Defines how qualification_threshold is interpreted.']
  qualification_period_days varchar [note: 'Number of days over which the qualification threshold must be met (e.g., 365 for annual qualification, 90 for quarterly). Null indicates lifetime accumulation.']
  qualification_threshold decimal(12,2) [note: 'Numeric threshold value required to qualify for this tier (e.g., 1000 points, 25 visits, $500 spend). Interpretation depends on qualification_metric.']
  referral_bonus_points varchar [note: 'Bonus points awarded to members in this tier for each successful referral of a new loyalty program member. Used to incentivize member acquisition through word-of-mouth.']
  rollover_points_allowed boolean [note: 'Indicates whether unused points can roll over to the next qualification period for members in this tier. True if rollover is allowed, false if points expire at period end.']
  sort_order varchar [note: 'Integer defining the hierarchical display order of tiers (e.g., 1 for Bronze, 2 for Silver, 3 for Gold, 4 for Platinum). Lower values represent entry-level tiers; higher values represent premium tiers.']
  target_member_segment varchar [note: 'Description of the target guest segment this tier is designed to attract and retain (e.g., frequent visitors, high spenders, digital-first guests, family diners). Used for marketing strategy and program design.']
  tier_status varchar [note: 'Current lifecycle status of the tier. Active tiers are available for member assignment; inactive tiers are temporarily disabled; retired tiers are no longer offered but may have legacy members; pending tiers are configured but not yet launched.']
  upgrade_notification boolean [note: 'Indicates whether members should receive automated notification when they qualify for upgrade to this tier. True if notification is enabled, false otherwise.']
  validity_days varchar [note: 'Number of days a member retains this tier status after qualification before re-evaluation (e.g., 365 for annual renewal). Null indicates permanent tier assignment.']
  welcome_bonus_points varchar [note: 'One-time bonus points awarded to a member upon first achieving this tier. Used to incentivize tier progression and enhance member engagement.']

  Note: 'Reference catalog of all loyalty membership tiers (e.g., Bronze, Silver, Gold, Platinum) defined in the Restaurants loyalty program. Stores tier name, tier code, qualification threshold (points or visit count), tier benefits summary, tier multiplier for points accrual, tier validity period, and sort order. Drives tier assignment logic and benefit entitlement across the program.'
}

Table loyalty.points_ledger {
  points_ledger_id varchar [pk, note: 'Unique identifier for each points transaction record in the loyalty ledger. Serves as the immutable primary key for this financial-grade system of record.']
  accrual_rule_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to loyalty.accrual_rule. Business justification: Each points accrual entry in the ledger is generated by a specific accrual rule (e.g., base earn rate, bonus multiplier rule, birthday bonus rule). Linking points_ledger.accrual_rule_id → loyalty.accr']
  loyalty_member_id varchar [note: 'Unique identifier of the loyalty program member whose points balance is affected by this transaction. Links to the member master record.']
  offer_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to loyalty.offer. Business justification: Bonus points accruals in the ledger are frequently tied to specific promotional offers (e.g., double points weekend, birthday bonus offer, new member enrollment offer). Linking points_ledger.offer_id ']
  primary_points_adjusted_by_user_employee_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the system user or customer service agent who authorized and executed the manual adjustment or reversal. Populated for adjust and reversal transactions. Critical for SOX compliance and fraud prevention.']
  primary_points_guest_order_id varchar [note: 'Reference to the originating order transaction that triggered this points movement. Populated for earn and redeem transactions tied to guest purchases. Null for non-order events such as bonus awards, expirations, or manual adjustments.']
  primary_points_restaurant_unit_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the restaurant location where this points transaction originated. Populated for POS and kiosk transactions. Null for OLO, mobile app, or admin transactions not tied to a specific physical location.']
  program_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the franchise entity that owns or operates the restaurant where this transaction occurred. Used for royalty calculations and franchise performance analytics. Null for company-owned locations or non-location transactions.']
  reversal_of_transaction_points_ledger_id varchar [note: 'Reference to the original points_ledger_id that this reversal transaction is canceling. Populated only for reversal transaction types. Creates an audit chain linking reversals to their source transactions.']
  reward_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the specific reward item redeemed by the member. Populated only for redeem transaction types. Links to the rewards catalog to identify what was claimed (free item, discount, experience).']
  source_transaction_guest_order_id varchar [note: 'The unique transaction identifier from the originating source system. Enables end-to-end traceability and reconciliation between the loyalty ledger and upstream systems. Format varies by source system.']
  tier_id varchar [note: 'Identifier of the loyalty membership tier the member held at the time of this transaction. Used to determine earn rates, bonus multipliers, and redemption privileges. Links to tier master data.']
  adjustment_reason_code varchar [note: 'Standardized code explaining why a manual adjustment or reversal was made. Populated only for adjust and reversal transaction types. Supports audit compliance and customer service quality tracking.']
  adjustment_reason_notes varchar [note: 'Free-text explanation provided by the customer service representative or system administrator who initiated the adjustment. Populated for adjust and reversal transactions. Supports audit trail and dispute resolution.']
  batch_reference varchar [note: 'Identifier of the processing batch or job that created this ledger entry. Used for bulk operations such as nightly expiration runs, mass bonus awards, or system migrations. Null for real-time individual transactions.']
  fiscal_period varchar [note: 'The fiscal period (month or accounting period number) within the fiscal year when this transaction occurred. Supports monthly financial close, P&L reporting, and loyalty liability accrual tracking.']
  fiscal_year varchar [note: 'The fiscal year in which this points transaction occurred, derived from transaction_timestamp. Supports financial reporting, GAAP revenue recognition for breakage, and year-over-year loyalty program analytics.']
  is_voided boolean [note: 'Flag indicating whether this transaction has been voided or reversed by a subsequent transaction. True if a reversal exists, false otherwise. Immutable ledger entries are never deleted, only marked as voided.']
  order_currency_code varchar [note: 'Three-letter ISO 4217 currency code for the order total amount. Supports multi-currency loyalty programs for international operations. Null for non-order transactions.']
  order_total_amount decimal(15,2) [note: 'The total monetary value of the order that triggered this points transaction, in the local currency. Used to calculate points earn rate and for financial reconciliation. Null for non-order transactions.']
  points_balance_after varchar [note: 'The cumulative running balance of points for this member immediately after this transaction was applied. Provides audit trail and reconciliation capability. Must equal prior balance plus points_delta.']
  points_delta varchar [note: 'The signed integer change in points balance for this transaction. Positive values represent accruals (earn, bonus), negative values represent deductions (redeem, expire, adjust downward). Zero-delta entries are not permitted.']
  points_earn_rate decimal(10,4) [note: 'The rate at which points were earned per currency unit spent. Example: 1.0000 means 1 point per dollar, 2.0000 means 2 points per dollar (double points promotion). Populated for earn transactions, null otherwise.']
  points_expiry_date date [note: 'The date on which the points earned in this transaction will expire if not redeemed. Populated only for earn and bonus transaction types. Null for redeem, expire, and adjust transactions. Supports FIFO expiration logic and member communication.']
  processed_timestamp timestamp [note: 'The date and time when this ledger entry was committed to the loyalty system of record. May differ from transaction_timestamp for batch processing or delayed reconciliation. Used for ETL audit and SLA monitoring.']
  restaurant_number varchar [note: 'Human-readable store or unit number for the restaurant location. Denormalized for operational reporting and guest service. Null for non-location-specific transactions.']
  source_channel varchar [note: 'The originating channel or interface through which this points transaction was initiated: POS (Point of Sale in-store), OLO (Online Ordering web platform), mobile_app (native mobile application), kiosk (self-service in-store kiosk), 3PD (Third-Party Delivery partner), call_center (phone order), admin (manual back-office adjustment). [ENUM-REF-CANDIDATE: pos|olo|mobile_app|kiosk|3pd|call_center|admin — 7 candidates stripped; promote to reference product]']
  source_order_number varchar [note: 'Human-readable order number or receipt identifier from the POS or OLO system. Provides guest-facing reference for customer service inquiries. Null for non-order transactions.']
  source_system_code varchar [note: 'Code identifying the operational system that originated this points transaction. Supports multi-system integration and data lineage tracking. Examples: micros_pos (Oracle MICROS POS), olo_platform (Olo Digital Ordering), salesforce_crm (Salesforce Marketing Cloud), loyalty_engine (core loyalty platform), admin_portal (back-office admin tool).']
  transaction_timestamp timestamp [note: 'The precise date and time when this points transaction occurred in the source system. Represents the business event time, not the ETL load time. Critical for sequencing and audit.']
  transaction_type varchar [note: 'Classification of the points movement: earn (points accrued from purchase or activity), redeem (points spent on rewards), expire (points lapsed due to inactivity or time limit), adjust (manual correction by support or system), bonus (promotional award), reversal (cancellation of prior transaction).']
  voided_timestamp timestamp [note: 'The date and time when this transaction was voided by a reversal. Null if the transaction has not been voided. Supports audit trail and reconciliation.']

  Note: 'Immutable financial-grade ledger recording every points movement for a loyalty member — accruals, redemptions, expirations, adjustments, bonus awards, goodwill credits, promotional credits, dispute resolutions, system error corrections, and fraud reversals. Each row captures transaction type (earn, redeem, expire, adjust, bonus, goodwill, reversal, correction), points delta, running balance, source channel (POS, OLO, 3PD), source order reference, campaign reference, expiry date of earned points, adjustment reason code, adjustment category (goodwill, dispute, error_correction, promotional, fraud), authorizing agent (for manual adjustments), approval status (auto_approved, pending_review, approved, rejected), processing timestamp, and related original transaction reference (for reversals/corrections). This is the single SSOT for ALL member point balance movements — no other product in this domain records points changes of any kind.'
}

Table loyalty.reward {
  reward_id varchar [pk, note: 'Unique identifier for the loyalty reward. Primary key.']
  brand_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to restaurant.brand. Business justification: Rewards are brand-specific in multi-brand restaurant enterprises (e.g., free item reward valid only at Brand X). Brand-level reward catalog management and cost-of-goods reporting require a structured ']
  combo_meal_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to menu.combo_meal. Business justification: Rewards can be redeemable combo meals (e.g., \'redeem 500 points for a combo meal\'). The reward product already links to menu_item but combo meals are a distinct product with bundle pricing and composi']
  employee_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to workforce.employee. Business justification: Campaign‑Reward mapping needed for tracking which marketing campaign created a specific reward, supporting performance analysis of campaign‑driven incentives.']
  menu_item_id varchar [note: 'Reference to the specific menu item associated with this reward (for food and beverage rewards). Links to the menu item master catalog. Null for non-menu rewards.']
  program_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to loyalty.program. Business justification: Allows franchisees to manage their own reward catalog, needed for localized promotions and compliance reporting.']
  supplier_contract_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to supply.supplier_contract. Business justification: Supplier-funded rewards (e.g., a supplier sponsors a free item reward for their product) require traceability to the specific supplier contract authorizing the funding. Restaurant chains use this link']
  supplier_id varchar [note: 'Foreign key linking to supply.supply_supplier. Business justification: Needed for Reward‑Supplier cost tracking and compliance; loyalty rewards are sourced from suppliers, and reporting requires linking each reward to its supplying supplier.']
  availability_end_date date [note: 'Date when this reward is no longer available for redemption. Null for evergreen rewards with no expiration. Used for Limited Time Offer (LTO) rewards and seasonal promotions.']
  availability_start_date date [note: 'Date when this reward becomes available for redemption by loyalty members. Used for Limited Time Offer (LTO) rewards and seasonal promotions.']
  code varchar [note: 'Externally-known unique alphanumeric code for the reward, used in Point of Sale (POS) and Online Ordering (OLO) systems for redemption lookup.']
  combinable_with_other_offers boolean [note: 'Indicates whether this reward can be combined with other promotional offers, coupons, or discounts in a single transaction. True if combinable, False if exclusive.']
  cost_of_goods_sold decimal(10,2) [note: 'Direct cost to the business to fulfill this reward, including food cost, packaging, and direct labor. Used for profitability analysis, loyalty program Return on Investment (ROI) calculation, and financial accrual. Expressed in USD.']
  created_timestamp timestamp [note: 'Timestamp when this reward record was first created in the system. Used for audit trail and data lineage tracking.']
  daypart_restriction varchar [note: 'Specifies which daypart(s) this reward can be redeemed during: breakfast, lunch, dinner, late night, or all day. Used to manage product mix (PMIX) and drive traffic during specific service periods.']
  description varchar [note: 'Detailed description of the reward, including terms, conditions, and guest-facing messaging used in marketing materials and the loyalty app.']
  discount_type varchar [note: 'Type of discount mechanism applied: percentage (e.g., 10% off), fixed amount (e.g., $5 off), free item (no charge), buy-one-get-one (BOGO), or none (for non-discount rewards like merchandise or experiences).']
  discount_value decimal(10,2) [note: 'Numeric value of the discount. For percentage discounts, this is the percentage (e.g., 15.00 for 15%). For fixed amount discounts, this is the dollar amount (e.g., 5.00 for $5 off). Null for free items and non-discount rewards.']
  featured_flag boolean [note: 'Indicates whether this reward is featured prominently in the loyalty app and marketing campaigns. True if featured, False if standard catalog placement. Used to drive awareness and redemption of strategic rewards.']
  format_restriction_list varchar [note: 'Comma-separated list of restaurant format types where this reward is valid (e.g., QSR, casual, fine-dining, food court). Null if applicable to all formats. Used when restaurant_applicability_scope is \'specific_formats\'.']
  image_url varchar [note: 'URL path to the reward image asset displayed in the loyalty app, Online Ordering (OLO) platform, and marketing materials. Used for visual merchandising and guest engagement.']
  market_restriction_list varchar [note: 'Comma-separated list of market codes or geographic regions where this reward is valid. Null if applicable to all markets. Used when restaurant_applicability_scope is \'specific_markets\'.']
  minimum_purchase_amount decimal(10,2) [note: 'Minimum transaction amount required to redeem this reward. Null if no minimum purchase is required. Used to protect Average Check Value (ACV) and prevent low-value transactions.']
  modified_by varchar [note: 'User identifier or system account that last modified this reward record. Used for audit trail and accountability.']
